In 5.1, redirecting a native command's stderr inside PowerShell wraps each line in an ErrorRecord (NativeCommandError) and sets `$?` to `$false` even when the exe returned exit code 0. stderr is already captured for you — don't redirect it.\n   - `>`, `>>`, and `Out-File` usually default to UTF-8 (with BOM) in this environment, but `Set-Content`/`Add-Content` still default to the system ANSI codepage — when writing a file other tools will read, pass `-Encoding utf8` explicitly to `Out-File`/`Set-Content`.\n   - `ConvertFrom-Json` returns a PSCustomObject, not a hashtable. `-AsHashtable` is not available.\n\nBefore executing the command, please follow these steps:\n\n1. Directory Verification:\n   - If the command will create new directories or files, first use `Get-ChildItem` (or `ls`) to verify the parent directory exists and is the correct location\n\n2. Command Execution:\n   - Always quote file paths that contain spaces with double quotes\n   - Capture the output of the command.\n\nPowerShell Syntax Notes:\n   - Variables use $ prefix: $myVar = \"value\"\n   - Escape character is backtick (`), not backslash\n   - Use Verb-Noun cmdlet naming: Get-ChildItem, Set-Location, New-Item, Remove-Item\n   - Common aliases: ls (Get-ChildItem), cd (Set-Location), cat (Get-Content), rm (Remove-Item)\n   - Pipe operator | works similarly to bash but passes objects, not text\n   - Use Select-Object, Where-Object, ForEach-Object for filtering and transformation\n   - String interpolation: \"Hello $name\" or \"Hello $($obj.Property)\"\n   - Registry access uses PSDrive prefixes: `HKLM:\\SOFTWARE\\...`, `HKCU:\\...` — NOT raw `HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE\\...`\n   - Environment variables: read with `$env:NAME`, set with `$env:NAME = \"value\"` (NOT `Set-Variable` or bash `export`)\n   - Call native exe with spaces in path via call operator: `& \"C:\\Program Files\\App\\app.exe\" arg1 arg2`\n\nUnix commands that DO NOT exist in PowerShell — use the equivalent instead:\n   - head / tail → `Get-Content file -TotalCount N` / `-Tail N`; piped: `| Select-Object -First N` / `-Last N`\n   - which → `(Get-Command name).Source`\n   - touch → `if (-not (Test-Path path)) { New-Item -ItemType File path }` (NEVER use `New-Item -Force` on a file — it truncates existing content)\n   - wc -l → `(Get-Content file | Measure-Object -Line).Lines`\n   - mkdir -p → `New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force path` (`-p` is not a PowerShell flag)\n   - rm -rf → `Remove-Item -Recurse -Force path`\n   - ln -s → `New-Item -ItemType SymbolicLink -Path link -Target target`\n   - chmod / chown → not applicable on Windows; use `icacls` only if ACL changes are required\n   - 2>/dev/null → `2>$null` (but stderr is captured for you — usually unnecessary)\n   - VAR=x cmd → `$env:VAR = 'x'; cmd` (PowerShell has no inline env-var prefix)\n   - Bash control flow (`if [ -f x ]`, `for x in *`, backtick ``cmd`` substitution) is a parser error — use `if (Test-Path x)`, `foreach ($x in ...)`, `$(cmd)`\n\nExit-code note: `-ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` suppresses error OUTPUT but the cmdlet failure still causes this tool to report exit 1. To make a cmdlet failure truly non-fatal, promote it to terminating and swallow it: `try { Cmdlet ... -ErrorAction Stop } catch {}` (without `-ErrorAction Stop`, non-terminating errors skip the `catch` and still exit 1).\n\nInteractive and blocking commands (this tool runs with -NonInteractive and stdin attached to the null device — console prompts read EOF or error immediately; GUI prompts can still block until timeout):\n   - NEVER use `Read-Host`, `Get-Credential`, `Out-GridView`, `$Host.UI.PromptForChoice`, or `pause`\n   - Destructive cmdlets (`Remove-Item`, `Stop-Process`, `Clear-Content`, etc.) may prompt for confirmation. Add `-Confirm:$false` when you intend the action to proceed. Use `-Force` for read-only/hidden items.\n   - Never use `git rebase -i`, `git add -i`, or other commands that open an interactive editor\n\nPassing multiline strings (commit messages, file content) to native executables:\n   - Use a single-quoted here-string so PowerShell does not expand `$` or backticks inside. The closing `'@` MUST be at column 0 (no leading whitespace) on its own line — indenting it is a parse error:\n<example>\ngit commit -m @'\nCommit message here.\nSecond line with $literal dollar signs.\n'@\n</example>\n   - Use `@'...'@` (single-quoted, literal) not `@\"...\"@` (double-quoted, interpolated) unless you need variable expansion\n   - For arguments containing `-`, `@`, or other characters PowerShell parses as operators, use the stop-parsing token: `git log --% --format=%H`\n\nUsage notes:\n  - The command argument is required.\n  - You can specify an optional timeout in milliseconds (up to 600000ms / 10 minutes for a foreground command). If not specified, commands will timeout after 120000ms (2 minutes).\n  - It is very helpful if you write a clear, concise description of what this command does.\n  - If the output exceeds 30000 characters, output will be truncated before being returned to you.\n  - You can use the `run_in_background` parameter to run the command in the background. Only use this if you don't need the result immediately and are OK being notified when the command completes later. You do not need to check the output right away - you'll be notified when it finishes.\n  - Avoid using PowerShell to run commands that have dedicated tools, unless explicitly instructed:\n    - File search: Use Glob (NOT Get-ChildItem -Recurse)\n    - Content search: Use Grep (NOT Select-String)\n    - Read files: Use Read (NOT Get-Content)\n    - Edit files: Use Edit\n    - Write files: Use Write (NOT Set-Content/Out-File)\n    - Communication: Output text directly (NOT Write-Output/Write-Host)\n  - When issuing multiple commands:\n    - If the commands are independent and can run in parallel, make multiple PowerShell tool calls in a single message.\n    - If the commands depend on each other and must run sequentially, chain them in a single PowerShell call (see edition-specific chaining syntax above).\n    - Use `;` only when you need to run commands sequentially but don't care if earlier commands fail.\n    - DO NOT use newlines to separate commands (newlines are ok in quoted strings and here-strings)\n  - Do NOT prefix commands with `cd` or `Set-Location` -- the working directory is already set to the correct project directory automatically.\n  - Avoid unnecessary `Start-Sleep` commands:\n    - Do not sleep between commands that can run immediately — just run them.\n    - If your command is long running and you would like to be notified when it finishes — simply run your command using `run_in_background`. There is no need to sleep in this case.\n    - Do not retry failing commands in a sleep loop — diagnose the root cause or consider an alternative approach.\n    - If waiting for a background task you started with `run_in_background`, you will be notified when it completes — do not poll.\n    - If you must poll an external process, use a check command rather than sleeping first.\n    - If you must sleep, keep the duration short to avoid blocking the user.\n  - For git commands:\n    - Prefer to create a new commit rather than amending an existing commit.\n    - Before running destructive operations (e.g., git reset --hard, git push --force, git checkout --), consider whether there is a safer alternative that achieves the same goal. Only use destructive operations when they are truly the best approach.\n    - Never skip hooks (--no-verify) or bypass signing (--no-gpg-sign, -c commit.gpgsign=false) unless the user has explicitly asked for it. It must be callable, accepting a `Mapping` object and returning a\r\n       boolean that indicates whether the value matches the query, and\r\n    2. it must have a stable hash that will be used for query caching.\r\n\r\n    In addition, to mark a query as non-cacheable (e.g. if it involves\r\n    some remote lookup) it needs to have a method called ``is_cacheable``\r\n    that returns ``False``.\r\n\r\n    This query protocol is used to make MyPy correctly support the query\r\n    pattern that TinyDB uses.\r\n\r\n    See also https://mypy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/protocols.html#simple-user-defined-protocols\r\n    \"\"\"\r\n    def __call__(self, value: Mapping) -> bool: ...\r\n\r\n    def __hash__(self) -> int: ...\r\n\r\n\r\nclass QueryInstance:\r\n    \"\"\"\r\n    A query instance.\r\n\r\n    This is the object on which the actual query operations are performed. The\r\n    :class:`~tinydb.queries.Query` class acts like a query builder and\r\n    generates :class:`~tinydb.queries.QueryInstance` objects which will\r\n    evaluate their query against a given document when called.\r\n\r\n    Query instances can be combined using logical OR and AND and inverted using\r\n    logical NOT.\r\n\r\n    In order to be usable in a query cache, a query needs to have a stable hash\r\n    value with the same query always returning the same hash. That way a query\r\n    instance can be used as a key in a dictionary.\r\n    \"\"\"\r\n\r\n    def __init__(self, test: Callable[[Mapping], bool], hashval: Optional[tuple]):\r\n        self._test = test\r\n        if hashval is not None:\r\n            try:\r\n                hash(hashval)\r\n            except TypeError:\r\n                # Contains something we can't freeze (e.g. a numpy array) --\r\n                # fall back to uncacheable rather than crash on first use.\r\n                hashval = None\r\n        self._hash = hashval\r\n\r\n    def is_cacheable(self) -> bool:\r\n        return self._hash is not None\r\n\r\n    def __call__(self, value: Mapping) -> bool:\r\n        \"\"\"\r\n        Evaluate the query to check if it matches a specified value.\r\n\r\n        :param value: The value to check.\r\n        :return: Whether the value matches this query.\r\n        \"\"\"\r\n        return self._test(value)\r\n\r\n    def __hash__(self) -> int:\r\n        # We calculate the query hash by using the ``hashval`` object which\r\n        # describes this query uniquely, so we can calculate a stable hash\r\n        # value by simply hashing it\r\n        return hash(self._hash)\r\n\r\n    def __repr__(self):\r\n        return 'QueryImpl{}'.format(self._hash)\r\n\r\n    def __eq__(self, other: object):\r\n        if isinstance(other, QueryInstance):\r\n            return self._hash == other._hash\r\n\r\n        return False\r\n\r\n    # --- Query modifiers -----------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n    def __and__(self, other: 'QueryInstance') -> 'QueryInstance':\r\n        # We use a frozenset for the hash as the AND operation is commutative\r\n        # (a & b == b & a) and the frozenset does not consider the order of\r\n        # elements\r\n        if self.is_cacheable() and other.is_cacheable():\r\n            hashval = ('and', frozenset([self._hash, other._hash]))\r\n        else:\r\n            hashval = None\r\n        return QueryInstance(lambda value: self(value) and other(value), hashval)\r\n\r\n    def __or__(self, other: 'QueryInstance') -> 'QueryInstance':\r\n        # We use a frozenset for the hash as the OR operation is commutative\r\n        # (a | b == b | a) and the frozenset does not consider the order of\r\n        # elements\r\n        if self.is_cacheable() and other.is_cacheable():\r\n            hashval = ('or', frozenset([self._hash, other._hash]))\r\n        else:\r\n            hashval = None\r\n        return QueryInstance(lambda value: self(value) or other(value), hashval)\r\n\r\n    def __invert__(self) -> 'QueryInstance':\r\n        hashval = ('not', self._hash) if self.is_cacheable() else None\r\n        return QueryInstance(lambda value: not self(value), hashval)\r\n\r\n\r\nclass Query(QueryInstance):\r\n    \"\"\"\r\n    TinyDB Queries.\r\n\r\n    Allows building queries for TinyDB databases. There are two main ways of\r\n    using queries:\r\n\r\n    1) ORM-like usage:\r\n\r\n    >>> User = Query()\r\n    >>> db.search(User.name == 'John Doe')\r\n    >>> db.search(User['logged-in'] == True)\r\n\r\n    2) Classical usage:\r\n\r\n    >>> db.search(where('value') == True)\r\n\r\n    Note that ``where(...)`` is a shorthand for ``Query(...)`` allowing for\r\n    a more fluent syntax.\r\n\r\n    Besides the methods documented here you can combine queries using the\r\n    binary AND and OR operators:\r\n\r\n    >>> # Binary AND:\r\n    >>> db.search((where('field1').exists()) & (where('field2') == 5))\r\n    >>> # Binary OR:\r\n    >>> db.search((where('field1').exists()) | (where('field2') == 5))\r\n\r\n    Queries are executed by calling the resulting object. They expect to get\r\n    the document to test as the first argument and return ``True`` or\r\n    ``False`` depending on whether the documents match the query or not.\r\n    \"\"\"\r\n\r\n    def __init__(self) -> None:\r\n        # The current path of fields to access when evaluating the object\r\n        self._path: tuple[Union[str, Callable], ...] = ()\r\n\r\n        # Prevent empty queries to be evaluated\r\n        def notest(_):\r\n            raise RuntimeError('Empty query was evaluated')\r\n\r\n        super().__init__(\r\n            test=notest,\r\n            hashval=(None,)\r\n        )\r\n\r\n    def __repr__(self):\r\n        return '{}()'.format(type(self).__name__)\r\n\r\n    def __hash__(self):\r\n        return super().__hash__()\r\n\r\n    def __getattr__(self, item: str):\r\n        # Generate a new query object with the new query path\r\n        # We use type(self) to get the class of the current query in case\r\n        # someone uses a subclass of ``Query``\r\n        query = type(self)()\r\n\r\n        # Now we add the accessed item to the query path ...\r\n        query._path = self._path + (item,)\r\n\r\n        # ... and update the query hash\r\n        query._hash = ('path', query._path) if self.is_cacheable() else None\r\n\r\n        return query\r\n\r\n    def __getitem__(self, item: str):\r\n        # A different syntax for ``__getattr__``\r\n\r\n        # We cannot call ``getattr(item)`` here as it would try to resolve\r\n        # the name as a method name first, only then call our ``__getattr__``\r\n        # method. \"\"\"\r\n        if not self._path and not allow_empty_path:\r\n            raise ValueError('Query has no path')\r\n\r\n        def runner(value):\r\n            try:\r\n                # Resolve    )\r\n\r\n    def any(self, cond: Union[QueryInstance, list[Any]]) -> QueryInstance:\r\n        \"\"\"\r\n        Check if a condition is met by any document in a list,\r\n        where a condition can also be a sequence (e.g. list).\r\n\r\n        >>> Query().f1.any(Query().f2 == 1)\r\n\r\n        Matches::\r\n\r\n            {'f1': [{'f2': 1}, {'f2': 0}]}\r\n\r\n        >>> Query().f1.any([1,         a list of which at least one document has to be contained\r\n                     in the tested document.\r\n        \"\"\"\r\n        if callable(cond):\r\n            def test(value):\r\n tested document.\r\n        \"\"\"\r\n        if callable(cond):\r\n            def test(value):\r\n                return is_sequence(value) and all(cond(e) for e in value)\r\n\r\n        else:\r\n The full suite passes: 238 tests, the existing ones plus the new ones. Nothing is committed yet.\n\n```python\nUser.age.between(18, 65)                       # 18 <= age <= 65\nUser.age.between(18, 65, include_upper=False)  # 18 <= age < 65\nUser.age.between(18, 65, include_lower=False, include_upper=False)\n```\n\nHow it meets the seven requirements:\n1. **Range query:** `between(lower, upper)` matches documents whose value falls in the range.\n2. **Inclusive by default:** both boundaries are included unless you say otherwise.\n3. **Exclusive boundaries:** set `include_lower=False` or `include_upper=False`, either one or both.\n4. **Lower above upper:** raises `ValueError` when the query is built, not when it runs.\n5. **Boundaries that can't be compared:** for example `between(1, 'z')` or `between(None, 5)`, these raise `TypeError`.\n6. **Missing or incomparable values:** documents with a missing field, a broken nested path, or a value that can't be compared (a string, `None`, a list or a dict against number boundaries) don't match. They never raise an error.\n7. **Nested queries, combinations and caching:** it works on nested paths, with `&`, `|` and `~`, and inside `any()` and `all()`. Two queries that differ only in their boundaries or in which ends are exclusive get different cache entries, so they never share cached results.\n\nIf both boundaries are equal and either end is exclusive, the query is allowed and simply matches nothing.\n\nThe new tests are in [tests/test_queries.py](tests/test_queries.py), plus one in [tests/test_tables.py](tests/test_tables.py) that runs the query against a real table and checks caching and cache invalidation."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":530,"cache_read_input_tokens":79444,"output_tokens":625,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":0},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":530,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":625,"cache_read_input_tokens":79444,"cache_creation_input_tokens":530,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":530},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard","fallback_credit":null},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"serverClassifierRequest":"f6aa6b8b-3ea1-462b-8548-559a11f1345c","apiBlockIndex":0,"requestId":"req_011CgjFsvXB1A8PUkazwd6Cw","requestRef":"790439e6-d002-4a3c-9d6e-760b305f4ac2","requestedModel":"claude-opus-5-5","attributionMcpServer":"ccd_turn","attributionMcpTool":"wrap_up","type":"assistant","uuid":"177879fd-6a6b-4c20-b01f-5d299a7ce667","timestamp":"2026-11-05T05:50:03.410Z","advisorModel":"claude-opus-5-5","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"C:\\Users\\risha\\Desktop\\AutoSE_Assignment_2\\experiment\\runs\\T2_A_2","sessionId":"21857a2d-5865-48b1-b308-030ad82d0569","version":"2.1.317","gitBranch":"main"}
